description: "Task list for the Windows Desktop Qualification feature"

# Tasks: Windows Desktop Qualification

**Input**: Design documents from `specs/001-desktop-qualification/`

**Prerequisites**: plan.md, spec.md, research.md, data-model.md, contracts/operations.md, quickstart.md, and checklists/. Every item of every checklist passes.

**Tests**: The spec (FR-028, SC-002 to SC-004) and the user request require tests. Contract tests come before the code that they test. Fixture tests in real disposable Git repositories come before any staging or commit code. No test passes by default.

**Organization**: Phases follow six batches: (1) shared scaffold and shell boot, (2) repository reads, (3) diff and staging correctness, (4) UI integration, (5) Windows qualification, (6) shell selection. Story phases carry `[USn]` labels. Setup, Foundational, and the cross-cutting qualification phases carry no label.

## Format: `[ID] [P?] [Story] Description`

- **[P]**: The task touches different files from the other `[P]` tasks in its group and depends on no incomplete task. A `[P]` group starts after all tasks above it finish. A sub-heading starts a new group.
- **Exception**: Batch 4 work may start after T090, before batch 3 ends. This covers the tests T118 to T122, T178, T184 to T187, T191, T239, T246, and T249. It also covers T258 to T260, T263, and T264. It also covers the frame tasks T123 to T128, T247, T248, and T250. It covers view group A (T129 to T141, T151) and T142 to T144. Each such task still waits for every task that it names in "Depends on". T142 to T144 wait for every group A task except T134, because none of them uses the comparison view.
- **Exception (batch 2 early start)**: T280 and T283 may run beside T164. They do not use M or L. T076 may start before the probes, with separate discovery calls. The merged batch 2 tests are T066 to T070, T072 to T075, T190, T204 to T209, and T252. After them, T285, T286, T078, T079, and T081 may start. They do not wait for T071, T192, T164, or the probes. Each such task still waits for every task that it names in "Depends on".
- **Exception (batch 3 early start)**: Track C4 may start T195, T198, T221, and T226 before T090. They are pure contract functions with contract tests, and they need no batch 2 code. They change only `src/contracts/file-action.ts`, `src/contracts/submodules.ts`, and their two test files. They import nothing from `src/git-service/` and change no exported schema. A Linux run qualifies nothing; the main session reruns them on Windows before the tick. Each such task still waits for every task that it names in "Depends on".
- **[Story]**: The user story from spec.md (US1 to US7).
- Each task names exact file paths.

## Path Conventions

- Paths follow the plan.md project structure: `src/frontend/`, `src/git-service/`, `src/contracts/`, `src-tauri/src/`, `src-tauri/capabilities/`, `src-tauri/binaries/`, `tests/contracts/`, `tests/git/`, and `tests/windows/`.
- Root files are `package.json`, `bun.lock`, `tsconfig.json`, `vite.config.ts`, `components.json`, and `.gitignore`.
- Feature evidence goes only into `specs/001-desktop-qualification/research.md` and the gate table in `specs/001-desktop-qualification/plan.md`.

## Test harness rules

These rules apply to every test task.

- Every test file calls `printOrigin` from `tests/origin.ts` for each `src/` module that it imports. A green run counts only when its `ORIGIN` lines point into the checkout under test.
- Each batch verification task names one control mutation. The mutation must turn the same command red. Undo the mutation, then rerun green.
- Git tests find Git at test time through `tests/git/git-binary.ts` and skip when Git is absent. A batch verification fails when a test skips for an absent Git.
- Some tests need a condition that the host cannot create, such as another account or a full volume. Such a test skips and names the missing capability. Record that case as `unverified` in research.md when the Windows run skips it.
- A Linux run qualifies nothing (constitution VI). A task is done only after a green Windows run.
- A cloud session runs the task commands and the control mutation on Linux. It reports the result. It does not mark the task done.
- At merge, the local session reruns the task commands and the control mutation on Windows, from the merged checkout.
- Only a green Windows rerun with `ORIGIN` lines in that checkout marks the task done. A red rerun keeps the task open.
- The shell double runs on Windows and Linux. Its capability is `shell-double`.
- The capability `symlink` names cases that need a symbolic link. It is present when `symlinkSync` succeeds at test time.
- A capability gate decides by exit status, never by Git message text. Example: the `no-enclosing-repository` gate (`noEnclosingRepository` in `tests/git/service-harness.ts`) runs `git -c safe.directory=* rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree` in the parent of the fixture root. Exit 0 means that an enclosing repository exists.
- `windows-path-rules`, `ntfs-junction`, `git-for-windows`, and `powershell` name Windows-only cases.
- A Linux skip needs no research.md record. Copy `OBSERVATION` lines into research.md only from Windows runs.
- Each cloud prompt states these rules. The session edits no task checkbox. It reports the pass, fail, and skip counts of each file, every `SKIP … missing capability` line, and the `ORIGIN` lines. It runs the control mutation on Linux, red and then green. It never changes a capability to make a case run. It reports each Linux behavior difference. It changes only the files of its track.
- Fixtures live only under the owned root from `tests/git/fixture-root.ts`. Tests never touch a developer repository or the user's Git configuration.
- No task commits an executable. Rust process tests re-execute the test binary. Tests find built or installed binaries at test time.
- Mutation assertions use `tests/git/git-oracle.ts`, not only the service parser. They compare full logical index entries, raw index digests, the SHA-256 of every fixture working file, refs, and operation markers.
- WSL tests find a WSL2 distro at test time through `tests/wsl/wsl-host.ts`. They skip with a named capability when one of these is absent: WSL, a WSL2 distro, or its systemd user manager. The same holds for `setsid` or Git 2.55.0 in the distro. The research.md file records each such skip as `unverified`. The Git floor test needs only a distro Git below 2.55.0, so it runs on the reference distro. Seam cases inject the probe result or hold the probe, so they need no Git 2.55.0. They run before the cases that need it (T207, T209). WSL fixtures live only under an owned `<home>/arbareto-fixtures-<random>` folder in the distro, never in `/tmp`. Each distro start empties `/tmp` (research.md R13). `<home>` is the output of `wsl.exe --distribution <distro> --exec /usr/bin/printenv HOME`, an argument array without a shell. Before it builds a fixture, `tests/wsl/wsl-host.ts` reads the file system type of that folder with `stat --file-system`. A type other than `ef53` gives the skip capability `wsl-fixture-fs-id`, because the app refuses that folder (contract §11). A step that restarts the distro first records the inode of its fixture folder. After the restart, the folder must exist with that inode. Otherwise the step stops with `fixture-lost` and counts no result. C17 (T245), F22 (T219), and T252 apply this check. WSL tests never install a package and never change WSL or distro configuration. They never run `--install`, `--update`, `--shutdown`, `--terminate`, or `--unregister`. One exception: T252 runs `--terminate` for the two dedicated test distros that `ARBARETO_WSL_ORDER_DISTROS` names, and for no other distro.
- A task that adds a test file under `tests/contracts`, `tests/git`, or `tests/wsl` adds it to `tests/groups.json` in the same change. A file that measures wall or CPU time, or asserts a wall-clock budget, goes into `serial` with its reason. Every other file goes into `parallel`. `bun run test:all` fails for a file in no group.
- Submodule tests use local bare remotes under the owned root. They enable local transport only through `protocol.file.allow=always` in the test's owned global configuration. The product never sets it, and no test fetches from the internet.
- A task that says "Report for research.md" does not edit research.md. Its test prints each record as one `OBSERVATION <task ID>: <fact>` line. A non-test task lists the record in its task report. The batch verification task copies these records into research.md. So parallel tasks never edit research.md together.

These rules apply to every task that creates a source file.

- Every new `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.rs`, and `.css` file under `src/`, `src-tauri/src/`, and `tests/` starts with two comment lines. They are `SPDX-License-Identifier: AGPL-3.0-or-later` and `Copyright (c) 2026 Ihor Lifanov` (NOTICE.md section 7(b)).
- JSON files have no comment syntax, so they carry no header.
- Generated or adapted shadcn files under `src/frontend/components/ui/` keep the shadcn MIT notice (research.md R9 "Frontend licence obligations").
- `src/frontend/index.css` gets the header in T124, because T123 needs the plain T005 import.
- Each batch verification task (T065, T090, T117, T149, T171, and T177) runs `rg -F --files-without-match "SPDX-License-Identifier: AGPL-3.0-or-later" src src-tauri/src tests -g "*.{ts,tsx,rs,css}" -g "!**/components/ui/**"`. It runs the same command with `Copyright (c) 2026 Ihor Lifanov`. Each command must print nothing. Before T124, T065, T090, and T117 add `-g "!**/index.css"`.

These rules apply to every task that changes a manifest or a lockfile, or builds a package.

- After T003 and T006, every dependency change needs a record in research.md R10 "Dependency reviews". A dependency change adds or removes a dependency, or changes its version, in `package.json` or `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`. A change to `bun.lock` or `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` is also a dependency change. A script-only `package.json` edit needs no record. The record follows plan.md "Tool and runtime boundaries".
- Each batch verification lists the manifest and lockfile changes since the previous batch verification with `git log --name-only`. It fails when a change has no record.
- Every task that builds a package records the tool versions that ran in research.md R10. Run `rustc --version`, `cargo --version`, `cargo tauri --version`, and `bun --version`. Take the MSVC toolset and Windows SDK from the T001 link probe. Run `& "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\tauri\NSIS\makensis.exe" /VERSION` after the build. Each version must be at least its plan.md minimum.

## Phase 1: Setup (Batch 1: shared scaffold and shell boot)

**Purpose**: Verify the toolchain and create the project skeleton.

- [x] T001 [P] Verify the existing toolchain without changes. Run `git version --build-options`, `bun --version`, `rustc --version`, `cargo --version`, and `cargo tauri --version`. Confirm the x64 `kernel32.Lib` path of the selected Windows SDK. In a scratch directory outside the repository, link `fn main(){}` with `rustc --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -C link-arg=/VERBOSE:LIB`. Read the MSVC toolset and Windows SDK versions from the `Searching` library paths, and record the `link.exe` banner separately. They must be at least MSVC toolset `14.51.36231` and Windows SDK `10.0.26100.0` (plan.md Technical Context). Record each version and the SDK path in `specs/001-desktop-qualification/research.md` R6. Do not install a duplicate tool.
- [x] T002 [P] Create `.gitignore` with `node_modules/`, `dist/`, `src-tauri/target/`, `src-tauri/gen/`, `src-tauri/binaries/*.exe`, and `src/frontend/generated/`. `tauri-build` writes capability schemas to `gen/schemas` (tauri-build lib.rs:17), and build output must not make the tree dirty. Create `src-tauri/binaries/.gitkeep` so that the sidecar output directory exists. No executable binary enters the repository.
- [x] T003 Create `package.json` with `"private": true`, `"type": "module"`, and caret ranges from plan.md Technical Context and research.md R9. Set `"version": "0.1.0"`, equal to the `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` version (T006). Set `"repository": { "type": "git", "url": "https://github.com/auric/arbareto" }`, the Corresponding Source location (plan.md About window). Each listed version is the lower bound. Runtime dependencies: `react` and `react-dom` `19.3.0`, `@tauri-apps/api` `2.12.0`, `@base-ui/react` `1.8.0`, `@pierre/diffs` `1.5.1`, `shiki` and `@shikijs/transformers` `4.4.3`, and `@tanstack/react-virtual` `3.14.13`. Development dependencies: `typescript` `7.0.2`, `@types/react` and `@types/react-dom` `19.3.0`, `vite` `8.3.1`, `@vitejs/plugin-react` `6.1.1`, `tailwindcss` and `@tailwindcss/vite` `4.3.3`, and `shadcn` `4.21.0`. Add `@types/bun` `~1.4.2`. Its lower bound equals the exact Bun runtime, and the range stays on that minor version. `bun.lock` records the exact version. Keep Bun itself exact. Set `"packageManager": "bun@1.4.2"` as documentation only; Bun enforcement of this field is not verified. The real check is the first step of `build:service`: `bun -e "if (Bun.version !== '1.4.2') { console.error('Bun 1.4.2 required, found ' + Bun.version); process.exit(1) }"`. It fails with a clear message when `Bun.version` differs from `1.4.2`. Define the scripts `build:frontend` (`bunx --bun vite build`), `build:service` (the Bun version check and the exact `bun build` command in quickstart.md §2, chained with `&&`), and `build` (`bun run build:frontend && bun run build:service`). Run `bun install` and commit `bun.lock`.
- [x] T004 [P] Create `tsconfig.json` for `src/` and `tests/` with `strict`, `target` `esnext`, `noEmit`, `module` `esnext`, `moduleResolution` `bundler`, and `jsx` `react-jsx`. Set `types` to `bun` and `vite/client`. Set `resolveJsonModule` to `true`, because T143 imports `src/frontend/generated/notices.json`. Set `skipLibCheck` to `true`, because `bun-types` and `vite/client` declare the same `ImportMeta` globals with conflicting types. This setting also skips our own `.d.ts` files, so `src/` and `tests/` hold no `.d.ts` file. TypeScript 7 rejects a CSS side-effect import without a declaration (TS2882), and `vite/client` declares CSS modules. Set `paths` to `{ "@/*": ["./src/frontend/*"] }` without `baseUrl`. The shadcn generator validates this alias, and the generated components import `@/components/ui/*`.
- [x] T005 [P] Create `vite.config.ts`, `src/frontend/index.html`, and `src/frontend/index.css` with only `@import "tailwindcss";`. The shadcn generator in T123 detected Tailwind v4 from that import (research.md R9). In `vite.config.ts`, resolve `root` to `src/frontend` and `build.outDir` to the repository-root `dist/` as absolute paths. Build both paths from `import.meta.url` with `fileURLToPath`. Vite resolves a relative `outDir` from `root`, so plain `dist` lands in `src/frontend/dist`. Set `build.emptyOutDir` to `true`, because Vite empties an `outDir` outside `root` only on request. Set `resolve.alias` `@` to the absolute `src/frontend` path. Vite does not read tsconfig `paths` by default. Add the React and Tailwind plugins and `worker.format` `es`. Bundle workers, Shiki grammars and themes, and WASM chunks locally. Reference no CDN URL.
- [x] T006 Create `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `src-tauri/build.rs`, and `src-tauri/Cargo.lock`. Set the package `version` to `0.1.0`. This one value feeds all three manifests. `tauri.conf.json` omits `version`, so Tauri reads it from `Cargo.toml` (tauri-utils config.rs:4101-4103, revision `447fa9f3f993fe77724189e355078b38ce20baea`). `package.json` repeats the same value (T003). The About window and the installer name use it. Declare `tauri` `2.12.0`, `tauri-build` `2.7.0`, `tauri-plugin-dialog` `2.8.0`, `windows` `0.62.0`, and `webview2-com` `0.39.1` as plain `x.y.z` requirements (the Cargo caret default). Declare `serde` `1.0.228` and `serde_json` `1.0.145` in the same form. Tauri's `Cargo.lock` selects these versions at revision `447fa9f3f993fe77724189e355078b38ce20baea` (research.md R7). Enable only these `windows` features (research.md R7 "Win32 binding features"): `Win32_Foundation`, `Win32_Security`, `Win32_Storage_FileSystem`, `Win32_System_IO`, `Win32_System_JobObjects`, `Win32_System_Pipes`, `Win32_System_SystemInformation`, `Win32_System_SystemServices`, `Win32_System_Threading`, `Win32_UI_Controls`, `Win32_UI_WindowsAndMessaging`, and `Wdk_System_SystemServices`. Record any added feature in research.md R10 "Dependency reviews". Add no shell, filesystem, clipboard, or updater plugin. Do not enable the `devtools` feature. In `build.rs`, register the 21 contract §3 operations through `tauri_build::AppManifest::commands`. Use the snake_case form of each operation name, such as `get_environment`, because Tauri names a command after its Rust function. tauri-build writes one `src-tauri/permissions/autogenerated/<command>.toml` file for each name. Commit these files, so that a build leaves the tree clean. Generate the lock with `cargo generate-lockfile --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, and commit `Cargo.lock`. Depends on T001.
- [x] T007 [P] Create `src-tauri/src/main.rs` and `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`. `main.rs` calls `run()`. `lib.rs` declares the modules `framing`, `jobs`, `spawn`, `env_sanitize`, `git_env`, `process_requests`, `bootstrap`, `service`, and `ipc` as empty files. `run()` builds the Tauri application with no command and no window. `main.rs` sets `#![cfg_attr(not(debug_assertions), windows_subsystem = "windows")]`, so a release build opens no console window. T265 adds an interim window, and T063 removes it.
- [x] T008 [P] Create `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`. Omit `version`, so Tauri takes `0.1.0` from `Cargo.toml` (T006). Set `productName` `Arbareto`, `identifier` `io.github.auric.arbareto`, `mainBinaryName` `arbareto`, `build.frontendDist` `../dist`, `build.beforeBuildCommand` `bun run build`, `bundle.targets` `["nsis"]`, `bundle.windows.nsis.installMode` `currentUser`, `bundle.windows.webviewInstallMode` `{ "type": "skip" }`, and `bundle.externalBin` `["binaries/arbareto-git"]`. Keep `app.windows` empty, because T063 creates the main window after the WebView2 check. Set `app.security.csp` to the directive map in research.md R9 "Content Security Policy". Keep the inline-style sources in `style-src-elem` and `style-src-attr` only. Add no `'unsafe-eval'` or `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` source. Set `bundle.windows.nsis.installerHooks` to `./installer-hooks.nsh`, and create `src-tauri/installer-hooks.nsh`. The file holds one `NSIS_HOOK_POSTINSTALL` macro with `WriteRegStr SHCTX "${UNINSTKEY}" "DisplayName" "${PRODUCTNAME} (Unsigned local test package)"`. The pinned template writes `DisplayName` from `productName` (installer.nsi:702) and runs this hook after that write (installer.nsi:734-735). It never reads `bundle.shortDescription` or `bundle.longDescription` (research.md R7). Do not change `productName` or `mainBinaryName` for the label. Remove the hook only when signing has verified evidence (FR-033). Create `src-tauri/icons/icon.ico`, and set `bundle.icon` to `["icons/icon.ico"]`. The pinned `tauri-build` takes the first `.ico` entry of `bundle.icon`, or else `icons/icon.ico` (lib.rs:798-810). It fails the build script with "`icons/icon.ico` not found" when that file is absent (lib.rs:860-869). On Windows, `tauri-codegen` takes the default window icon from that resource (context.rs:211-215). The file is an inert data file, not an executable. It holds one 16×16 32-bit image of one solid color with an ICONDIR header and no logo art. Write its bytes with a one-off Bun command, and commit only the `.ico` file. The NSIS installer does not need it: the pinned bundler reads only the optional `installerIcon` and `uninstallerIcon` (nsis/mod.rs:346-351, installer.nsi:131-134, :158-161).
- [x] T009 [P] Create `src-tauri/capabilities/main.json`. Grant the main local window only the 21 command permissions from T006 and the core event permission for service events. Grant no remote URL, no `shell:*` permission, no filesystem permission, and no dialog JavaScript permission.

**Checkpoint**: The project skeleton exists with minimum-version manifests, an exact Bun version, and committed lockfiles. No command runs a test yet.

## Phase 2: Foundational (Batch 1)

**Purpose**: Contracts, framing, process ownership, service transport, and the Git test harness. Every story depends on this phase.

**CRITICAL**: No story work starts before this phase is complete.

### Harness and contract tests (write first; they must fail)

- [x] T010 Create `tests/origin.ts`. Export `printOrigin(specifier, fromDir)`, which prints `ORIGIN <absolute path>` through `Bun.resolveSync`. Apply the test harness rules above to every later test task.
- [x] T011 [P] Write failing tests in `tests/contracts/strict-json.test.ts` for `src/contracts/strict-json.ts`. Assert `INVALID_REQUEST` for duplicate keys at any depth, nesting deeper than 16 levels, invalid UTF-8, trailing data, and integers outside the safe range. Assert that valid nested input decodes to the same value as `JSON.parse`.
- [x] T012 [P] Write failing tests in `tests/contracts/envelope.test.ts` for `src/contracts/envelope.ts` and `src/contracts/operations.ts`. A `version` other than integer `1` gives `PROTOCOL_VERSION`. Identifiers must be UUID-shaped ASCII strings of at most 64 bytes. An operation outside the 21 names gives `UNKNOWN_OPERATION`. Only `currentSession` omits `sessionId`; every other operation without it gives `INVALID_REQUEST`. `repositoryGeneration` is required for `readHistory`, `readComparison`, `applyHunk`, `applyFile`, `reviewCommit`, `commit`, `reviewSubmodules`, `updateSubmodules`, and `approveTrust`. It is optional for `readWorkspace` and absent for the other operations. Unknown payload fields, executable paths, patch text, ref expressions, and traversal strings give `INVALID_REQUEST`. An encoded request above 1 MiB gives `FRAME_LIMIT`. `requestClose` accepts only `stay`, `closeIfIdle`, and `interruptAndClose`. For `chooseGit`, `chooseRepository`, and `restoreWorkspace`, the renderer validator accepts only an empty object. Their service validator accepts only the native-issued `bootstrapId`, plus a fully qualified `executablePath` for `chooseGit` (contract §3). For `dismissRecoveryMarker`, the renderer validator accepts only a marker ID. Its service validator accepts only that marker ID and a `bootstrapId`. The `applyHunk` and `applyFile` directions accept only `stage` and `unstage`. `applyFile` accepts only a comparison ID, a snapshot ID, and the direction, and it rejects a path field. `readWorkspace` accepts only a boolean `refresh` and a `check` of `full` or `changes`, and `refresh: true` requires `check: "full"`. `readHistory` accepts only an empty payload. `reviewSubmodules` accepts only a snapshot ID. `updateSubmodules` accepts only a submodule review token and an explicit start, and it rejects a path field.
- [x] T013 [P] Write failing tests in `tests/contracts/operation-states.test.ts` for `src/contracts/outcome.ts`. Cover every row of the data-model §7 transition table. Assert that `unknown` stays blocked after a status-only success. Assert that a recovery refresh that completes recovery reconciliation (contract §8) makes the workspace `ready`. This holds also when attribution stays `unknown`, and prior tokens stay invalid. Assert that a failed or limited observation during that refresh keeps the workspace `unknown`. Assert that confirmed intended effects after a late cancellation give `succeeded`. Assert that a canceled read refreshes relevant state and ends `canceled`. Assert that a read deadline ends `failed` with `DEADLINE`. Assert the contract §9 result code of each terminal state, each by name. `canceled` carries `CANCELED`, `completed-with-effects` carries `COMPLETED_WITH_EFFECTS`, and `failed-with-effects` carries `FAILED_WITH_EFFECTS`. Assert that a mutation outcome carries `lingeringDescendants` as a non-negative integer (contract §6 "Lingering Jobs"). A count of 1 keeps `succeeded`. Cover the rows from 2026-09-30 one by one. A mutation step, preflight read, or commit deadline closes the Job and enters `reconciling` with cause `DEADLINE`. Reconciliation then classifies the outcome from observed state. A cancellation during `reconciling` keeps the reconciliation reads running and sets `endProcesses` on `ended`, and `lingeringDescendants` is 0. A reconciliation read deadline gives `unknown` and keeps the marker. Interrupt and close during `reconciling` sends no terminal response and keeps the marker. `revokeTrust` for a terminal mutation with a lingering Job returns the count of ended processes. `revokeTrust` during a running mutation cancels nothing and sets `endProcesses`. `limited` carries `OUTPUT_LIMIT` or `OBJECT_MISSING` (contract §9).

The bridge calls `currentSession` without a `sessionId`. It exports no generic invoke function. An `operation-progress` event with the `requestId` of a pending request gives that request its `operationId` and calls its progress handler. An event for an unknown or settled `requestId` calls no handler. A request rejected before admission resolves with no `operationId`. Note: T269 supersedes the injected double of this task. The tests use `mockIPC`.

## Phase 3: User Story 1 - Install and use a Windows workspace, boot part (Priority: P1) (Batch 1)

**Goal**: The installed package boots and checks WebView2 before any webview exists. It admits Git from the minimum version and reports the platform scope. Blocked states show the detected and minimum versions.

**Independent Test**: Install the batch 1 package as the current user. Start it with admitted, missing, and below-minimum Git. The boot view shows the admission result and the platform scope. No blank window appears.

## Batch 1 follow-up: IPC migration

**Purpose**: Move the renderer IPC and the contract types to zod 4. See contract §1 "Renderer request body" and "Schemas", and plan.md "Contract schemas". The user approved this design on 2026-10-04. T266 to T279 supersede the transport parts of T029, T037, T047, and T188.

The renderer envelope is a discriminated union of the 21 operations, and the service envelope of 19. Each operation accepts its exact payload and refuses an unknown field. `validateRequest` returns `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, then `UNKNOWN_OPERATION`, then `INVALID_REQUEST`, in that order. A `version` of `1.0` from the strict decoder gives `PROTOCOL_VERSION`. A commit message of exactly 65,536 UTF-8 bytes that ends in a 4-byte character is valid. One more byte is invalid. The schemas also cover the implemented results, the progress event, the handshake, and the `process`, `mutation`, and `trust` frames. A trust record and a `confirm` frame hold `gitSelectedPath`, a channel path, and no `gitInstallationDigest`.

## Phase 4: Batch 2: Repository reads (US2, US5, US6)

**Goal (US2)**: The service opens repositories and linked worktrees and returns identity, status, history, and trust facts that match Git.

**Independent Test (US2)**: `bun test tests/git` builds F01 to F03, F05 to F08, F10, and F15 to F19. It compares service results with construction facts and with `tests/git/git-oracle.ts`.

**Goal (US5)**: Reads stay cancelable and bounded. Obsolete results never replace newer state. External changes invalidate approvals.

**Independent Test (US5)**: Barrier tests hold real Git reads, cancel them, finish them out of order, and change fixtures externally.

**Goal (US6)**: WSL paths route to the distro Git. Distro checks, the Git floor, the start step, and Linux process ownership hold.

**Independent Test (US6)**: `bun test tests/wsl` builds F21 and F24 to F26 in a WSL2 distro. It compares service results with the distro Git and lists Linux processes with `pgrep`. Without the prerequisites, only the F24 floor case and the seam cases run.

A status read that needs a missing blob ends `limited` with `OBJECT_MISSING` and the contract §7 remedy text. An unstaged comparison whose index blob is missing gives `OBJECT_MISSING` and no hunk, although `ls-files --eol` exits 0 with `i/none`. The runner saw `--no-lazy-fetch` first in every argument array, the `cat-file --batch-check` object check included. `readTrust` lists traditional hooks, and `git hook list -z --show-scope` registrations with disabled ones included. It also lists conditional includes, worktree configuration, filters, monitors, diff tools, and signing programs. Put a secret canary in `http.extraheader` and in a remote URL with user information. `readTrust` returns their key, scope, and origin, and neither canary value appears (contract §7 "Value display allowlist"). Trust `state` is "`untrusted`, `approved`, `changed`, or `revoked`." A configuration change after approval gives `TRUST_CHANGED` and invalidates dependent approvals. Persistence (contract §7 "Trust approval store"): `approveTrust` sends `confirm`, and a declined confirmation ends `canceled` with no record. After an accepted approval, a new service session with the same record store needs no approval. A changed fingerprint, selected Git path, or injected common-directory identity gives `TRUST_CHANGED`. A selected Git path that differs only in ASCII letter case keeps the approval (contract §7). A changed Git digest behind the same selected path keeps the approval. Only an approval of the new fingerprint clears it. Without required approval, a changed fingerprint clears after the next `readWorkspace` and a new selection. `revokeTrust` deletes the record, and the next session reports `untrusted`. A configuration listing above 4 MiB blocks approval. Program digests follow contract §7 "Program file digests". Give F17 a repository `pre-commit` hook and a local clean filter program `./tools/clean.sh`, and approve it. Change one byte of the hook file, with no configuration change. The next `readTrust` reports trust `state` `changed`, with `commit` approval required. T098 covers the `commit` refusal. Restore the byte in a new session; the stored approval matches again. Change one byte of the filter program, and reads give `TRUST_CHANGED`. A repository-controlled program file above 1 MiB makes `readTrust` report the limit and `persistence` `session`. So do 65 repository-controlled program files, and nine files of 1 MiB each (above the 8 MiB total). In each over-bound case, `approveTrust` accepts an explicit approval, sends `confirm` with `persist: false`, and the store writes no record. Reads then run in that session. An edit of an `unchecked` file keeps the session approval. A new service session reports `untrusted` and gives `TRUST_REQUIRED` again. This holds for a handshake with `restarted: true`, as after Restart service. An over-bound approval after an earlier stored approval deletes that stale record, so the next session reports `untrusted`, not `changed`. A listed in-repository program path that is absent or not a regular file enters the fingerprint as absent. Creating that file gives `TRUST_CHANGED`. A local filter program with an absolute owned-root path outside the repository gets no digest. An edit of that file keeps the fingerprint. Bare-name lookup (contract §7): set the child `PATH` of the owned service environment to `tools;<absolute owned bin>`. Give F17 a local clean filter with the bare name `cleaner`. Without `tools\cleaner.exe` and `tools\cleaner`, the fingerprint records `absent` for `tools`. Creating `tools\cleaner` gives `TRUST_CHANGED`. After approval, one changed byte of `tools\cleaner` gives `TRUST_CHANGED`. With both `tools\cleaner.exe` and `tools\cleaner` present, the digest covers `tools\cleaner.exe`, as Git for Windows tries `.exe` first. A file in the absolute owned bin directory gets no digest. `revokeTrust` does not cancel running work. It returns the process count from the `revoke` reply (T100 covers the lingering Jobs). A `core.hooksPath` from `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` appears in `readTrust` with scope `command`. With `GIT_CONFIG` pointing to an owned file, a local filter canary still gives `TRUST_REQUIRED`, and the canary never runs. `readTrust` reports `approvalRequired` separately for reads and for `commit`. A repository `pre-commit` hook sets it for `commit` only. So does a local `gpg.ssh.defaultKeyCommand` with `gpg.format=ssh`, `commit.gpgsign=true`, and no `user.signingKey`, and `readTrust` shows its value. An owned global `core.hooksPath=.githooks` with a committed `pre-commit` canary makes `readTrust` report `commit` approval required. The canary never runs before approval. A global `hook.<name>.command` with a relative program gives the same result. A global `filter.<driver>.clean` or `core.fsmonitor` with a relative program blocks reads with `TRUST_REQUIRED`. A global `include.path` to a file inside the worktree makes its keys repository-controlled. F07 per-worktree records: set `extensions.worktreeConfig=true`. Give linked worktrees A and B a worktree-scope clean filter each, with different programs. Approve A, then approve B. A new session reports both `approved`, and approving B left the record of A unchanged. One changed byte of the B program gives `TRUST_CHANGED` for B only; A stays `approved`. `revokeTrust` from A makes both report `untrusted` in the next session. Stale record: approve F17, then remove every repository-controlled integration. The stored record keeps trust `state` `changed`, and `approvalRequired` is false for reads and `commit`. After the next `readWorkspace` and a new selection, reads and `applyHunk` run without `TRUST_CHANGED` (contract §7 "Mutation trust").

## Batch 2 follow-up: portability guard

**Purpose**: Keep shared code portable while only Windows runs the app (user decision, 2026-10-04). Move the Windows path rules behind one TypeScript boundary, and compile the Win32 shell code only for Windows. A scan test and a Linux `cargo check` guard both. This is no port and no support claim. See contract §6 "Channel frames" and plan.md "Contract schemas".

### Tests for the portability guard (write first; they must fail)

It refuses `x`, `C:x`, `\x`, `/x`, `\\server`, `\\server\`, `\\.`, `\\.\pipe\x`, `\\?\C:\x`, `//./pipe/x`, and `//?/C:/x`. `PATH_RULES.posix.isAbsolute` accepts `/` and `/x`, and refuses `x`, `C:\x`, and `\\server\share`. `isGitExecutable` accepts `C:\Git\cmd\GIT.EXE` and refuses `C:\Git\cmd\git.cmd` on Windows. ## Batch 2 follow-up: file facts and merged refusal

**Purpose**: Base trust and discovery only on authoritative facts (user decision, 2026-10-05). The new shell request `fileFacts` reads program and hook files below the three Git-resolved roots. It never follows a link below a root. The service never stats, opens, or reads a repository-controlled program or hook path. Every nonzero exit of the first discovery call gives one refusal, `REPOSITORY_UNAVAILABLE`. See spec.md Clarifications, Session 2026-10-05. Track L3 runs T300 and T301 first. Track L2 runs the shell tasks, and cloud track C6 runs the service and double tasks. L1 reruns each C6 merge on Windows (Test harness rules).

It refuses `""`, `.`, `..`, `a\b`, `a/b`, `x:y`, `a<b`, `nul`, `COM1.txt`, `x.`, `x ` with a trailing space, and U+0001. `PATH_RULES.posix.isComponent` accepts `x:y` and `nul`. It refuses `""`, `.`, `..`, `a/b`, and NUL. Add the valid vectors `valid/file-facts-request.json` with the three roots and `valid/file-facts-reply.json` with one fact of each kind. Add invalid vectors for these cases: a component `..`, and a component with `\` and `"platform": "windows"`. Also add 65 entries, the root `home`, a request without `digest`, and a `symlink` link without `target`. Also add a `file` fact with both `digest` and `bound`, and an upper-case digest. ## Phase 5: Batch 3: Diff and staging correctness (US2, US3, US4, US5, US6, US7)

**Goal (US2)**: Canonical comparisons carry support decisions and explicit limits.

**Goal (US3)**: Exactly one verified hunk publishes, with an exact index and unchanged working-file bytes. Whole-file staging gives the index of Git's own whole-file staging.

**Goal (US4)**: One ordinary Git commit runs after a fresh preflight, and its reconciled outcome is truthful.

**Goal (US5)**: Interrupted mutations reconcile. Unknown state blocks mutations. A restart replays nothing. A silent commit or submodule step shows the stall notice.

**Goal (US6)**: Hunk, file, and commit mutations in a WSL repository give the results of the distro Git.

**Goal (US7)**: The submodule review and update give the results of Git's own recursive update. They refuse unsafe states and confirm submodule programs first.

**Independent Test**: `bun test tests/git` runs the comparison, hunk, file-staging, commit, and lifecycle tests. They use F01 to F07, F09 to F13, F16, F17, F20, and F27 to F30. Every mutation test asserts exact index entries and working-file digests through the oracle.

### Fixtures and probes

- [x] T091 [US3] Extend `tests/git/fixtures.ts` with fixture builders. They cover the F02 hunk variants, F04, F09, F11 to F14, F16 (comparison part), and F20. F02 variants: two unstaged hunks plus staged changes in the same file and unrelated staged content. A second variant has two staged hunks plus unstaged edits. F04: LF, CRLF, and mixed files under each `core.autocrlf` value, `core.eol`, `core.safecrlf`, and the `text`, `text=auto`, `-text`, `eol`, and `crlf` attributes. F11: traditional and configured hooks, disabled registrations, `post-index-change`, command-valued fsmonitor, and changing and rejecting commit hooks. F12 adds a repository with local `gc.auto=1` and loose objects above that threshold, so a commit triggers automatic maintenance. F12 also adds an SSH signing case: `gpg.format=ssh`, `commit.gpgsign=true`, no `user.signingKey`, and a local `gpg.ssh.defaultKeyCommand` canary. The F16 comparison part holds one file with 1,048,576-byte sides and one with 1,048,577 bytes. It also holds changes whose canonical diff has 20,000 and 20,001 lines. One more change has above 4 MiB of patch bytes. F14 builders serve T153, T163, and T171. Slow Git comes from a `pre-commit` hook that sleeps 600 seconds. Another `pre-commit` hook starts a descendant process. The orphaned-grandchild variant starts a grandchild that holds no inherited pipe handle. The grandchild writes its Windows PID and creation time into the owned root and sleeps 60 seconds. The hook then exits. The monitor variant sets local `core.fsmonitor=true` and starts no daemon. The lingering variant has a `post-commit` hook that starts a background child and exits. The child writes its PID and creation time into the owned root and sleeps 60 seconds. The lock variant's child first creates `index.lock` at the Git-resolved path, then sleeps. Hooks, barriers, and canaries live under the owned root. F02 adds a racy variant. An unrelated tracked file changes with the same size. The change falls in the second of the last index write (contract §4 "Candidate time"). F10 adds `core.splitIndex=true` at local and at global scope with an unsplit index, and a split index from `update-index --split-index`. Then probe trace2 (unverified). Run a failing `pre-commit` hook and a failing signing program with `GIT_TRACE2_EVENT` pointed at an owned file. Record in research.md R4 whether the `child_start` and `child_exit` events identify each child and its exit status. Also record whether the variable replaces a configured `trace2.eventTarget`.

Each hunk is "`hunkId`, ordinal, old/new ranges, exact canonical hunk digest, and service-owned application data."

Compare the on-disk name with the index path bytes only for changed entries or at request time. Give `path` on any difference or read failure. Check the size limits before decoding. **Checkpoint**: Batch 3 is complete. Hunk, file staging, commit, and submodule update proof cases pass in real repositories. The UI can now enable mutation controls (plan.md qualification sequence step 6).

---

## Phase 6: Batch 4: UI integration (US1, US2, US3, US4, US5, US6, US7)

**Goal (US1)**: A compact workbench frame with tokens, both themes and densities, layout profiles, resizable panes, keyboard rules, menus, and the About window.

**Goal (US2)**: Changes lists, the comparison view, the History graph, trust approval, repository states with the notice band, and per-mode context.

**Goal (US3)**: Explicit hunk controls bound to service hunk IDs, and file controls and the Space key bound to the file-action decision.

**Goal (US4)**: Draft rules, the commit tray, and the commit review dialog.

**Goal (US5)**: Request freshness, operation status with the status area, the close prompt, and component-stopped handling.

**Goal (US6)**: The WSL location label, the Starting WSL distro state with Cancel, WSL refusal texts, and focused polls.

**Goal (US7)**: The submodule notice in the notice band, the review dialog, the progress, and the outcome in the status area.

**Independent Test**: `bun test tests/contracts tests/git` passes the renderer-state and lane tests. The installed build shows F02 in both categories and stages one hunk by keyboard, as T149 describes.

## Phase 7: Qualification setup (Batch 5: Windows qualification, cross-cutting)

**Purpose**: Create the qualification register, the clean-install environment, the UI Automation driver, the ratio script, and the F32 builder. The installed-app launcher T151 moved to batch 4, because T149 uses it.

- [ ] T150 Add the subsection "Scenario records" under "R10. Research conclusion and qualification register" in `specs/001-desktop-qualification/research.md`, after the evidence-group table. R10 requires that the records stay there, so add no separate section or report. Add one table row for each of SC-001 to SC-017, F01 to F32, and UI-01 to UI-19: 68 rows. Columns: ID, result (`pass`, `fail`, or `unverified`), build and package digest, environment, fixtures, and steps. Further columns: actual result, evidence path and digest, failure cause, attempted remedy, retest, and decision impact. Every row starts as `unverified`. The SC-004 row takes the T117 classification record from R4 when one exists. A row whose only evidence is source inspection stays `unverified`, and its evidence column names `source-inspected` (data-model §8). Each record also holds the data-model §8 fields. They start with "Scenario identifier, application revision, release profile, package digest, and unsigned or signed package status". They end with "Raw evidence location and digest where applicable. Redact secrets before retaining evidence." Record results as numbers and observations, never as comparative adjectives.
- [ ] T152 [P] Prepare the clean-install environment: a disposable clean Windows 11 x64 environment, a Hyper-V VM or Windows Sandbox. Choose one option before batch 5. Record the choice, the OS image, and the settings in research.md R10. For Hyper-V, use 4 virtual CPUs, 8 GiB RAM, and a 64 GiB disk. For Windows Sandbox, the user enables the Windows Sandbox feature once, with administrator rights and a reboot. Use a `.wsb` file with `<Networking>Disable</Networking>` and a read-only mapped folder for the installer. Sandbox keeps no state between sessions, so install the prerequisites in each session. A `.wsb` logon command may script this. Before you rely on Sandbox, verify and record two facts. First, check whether a standard (non-administrator) account can run the install. Second, check whether the Sandbox image already contains WebView2. If either check fails, use Hyper-V for the affected tasks. Install Git for Windows `2.55.0.windows.3` or later and WebView2 `154.0.4258.37` or later, then keep the environment offline. When the image has an older runtime, update it with Microsoft's Evergreen Standalone Installer, and record its digest (research.md R7). Install no Bun or Node on it.
- [ ] T179 [P] Create `tests/windows/contrast.ts` and `tests/windows/contrast.test.ts`. `bun tests/windows/contrast.ts <capture.png> <regions.json>` decodes an 8-bit RGB or RGBA PNG without interlace. Use `node:zlib`, and refuse other formats. Each region names a plan.md token pair, a kind (`text` or `non-text`), and a pixel box. The background is the most frequent color in the box. The foreground is the box pixel with the highest contrast against that background. The script prints the WCAG 2.2 contrast ratio of each region with two decimals. It exits non-zero for a `text` region below 4.5:1 or a `non-text` region below 3:1. The test builds PNG files in memory and computes chunk CRCs with `Bun.hash.crc32`. It asserts 21.00:1 for `#000000` on `#FFFFFF` and 5.06:1 for `#576171` on `#DCE8FA`. Add no dependency.
- [ ] T240 [P] Create the test-only UI Automation driver `tests/windows/uia.ts` and its test `tests/windows/uia.test.ts`. The driver creates `CUIAutomation` with `CoCreateInstance` and calls the `IUIAutomation` interfaces through `bun:ffi`, like `inject.ts`. It needs no installation. It exports `find(processId, name, timeoutMs)`. That function searches below the process's top-level window for an element whose UI Automation Name equals `name`. It exports `invoke(element)`, which calls the Invoke pattern. It exports `readText(element)`, which returns the Text pattern text, or the joined Names of the descendants without that pattern. It exports `waitFocus(processId, name, timeoutMs)`, which returns the QueryPerformanceCounter value when the focused element has that Name. Every wait polls at most every 5 ms and stops at its bound, at most 10 seconds. UI Automation does not connect processes that different users start (Microsoft Learn "UI Automation Overview"). So the driver runs under the account that runs the app. The test starts the installed app with `tests/windows/installed-app.ts` (T151) and skips with a reason when it is absent. It finds "Open repository" and invokes it, then finds the folder picker's Cancel button and invokes it. It reads the status region text. It asserts that no file under `src/` imports `bun:ffi` or `uia.ts`. Depends on T151.
- [ ] T243 [P] Create the test-only script `tests/windows/ratio.ts` and its test `tests/windows/ratio.test.ts` (plan.md §Matched-condition comparisons). Write the test first; it must fail. `bun tests/windows/ratio.ts <runs.json>` reads the counted runs of each application, each with its time and its run uncertainty u. It adds u to each Arbareto time and subtracts u from each Git Extensions time. It prints both medians, the ratio, and the percentile bootstrap 95% interval. A runs file can mark a block that the plan.md "Invalid capture" rule ended, with its application and cause. The script then prints `missed` for an Arbareto block ended by no region change. It prints `unverified` for every other ended block, and no interval. The bootstrap draws 10,000 resamples from a 32-bit xorshift generator seeded with 42, not `Math.random`. Each resample draws each application's runs with replacement at their original count. The median of an even count is the mean of the two middle values. The script exits non-zero when the upper bound exceeds the target that the file names. The test asserts that equal samples give the ratio 1 and the interval [1, 1]. It asserts that the same input gives the same interval. It asserts that a positive u raises the ratio. It asserts the medians and the ratio of one hand-computed case with three runs per application. It asserts the median of one five-run case, which is the third value. It asserts `missed` for an Arbareto block ended by no region change, and `unverified` for the same Git Extensions mark. Add no dependency.

## Phase 9: Release-build measurement protocol (Batch 5, cross-cutting)

**Purpose**: Run the fixed plan.md measurement protocol (FR-030, SC-005 to SC-008, SC-010, SC-015) on the installed release build. Change no budget.

- [ ] T165 Record the measurement environment in research.md R10. Record the reference host (Ryzen 7 5800H, nominal 32 GiB RAM, Samsung 970 EVO Plus), Windows `10.0.26200.9457`, and the drivers. Record the WebView2 and Git versions installed at run time, and the Defender engine and signature versions. Each prerequisite version must be at least its admission minimum. Record AC power, Balanced mode without battery saver, the thermal state, and the background activity. Keep Defender enabled. Set the Windows setting Animation effects to Off for the measurement account, and record it (plan.md §Matched-condition comparisons). Set Arbareto to Compact density and the Windows Text size to 100%, and record both. The admission minimums are Git `2.55.0.windows.3` and WebView2 `154.0.4258.37`, the reference host versions on 2026-09-29. Record the Git and WebView2 versions before and after each series. A version change voids the series. Before T166 starts, write the failing test `tests/windows/inject.test.ts`, then create the test-only script `tests/windows/inject.ts`. The script calls the Windows `SendInput` API through `bun:ffi` and needs no installation (research.md R2 and R6). For each input, it records the QueryPerformanceCounter value at injection. It exports its QPC function for T242 and T244, and it calls `SendInput` only from its command-line entry. The test injects one Shift down and up pair. It asserts that `SendInput` reports two inserted events and that the two records have nondecreasing counter values. It also asserts that no file under `src/` imports `bun:ffi` or `inject.ts`. Take presented-frame timing from the WPR trace with GPU and DXGI present events. The plan.md section "Measurements and budgets" fixes the profile, provider, markers, and frames. They are the built-in `GPU` profile with `tests/windows/arbareto.wprp` (T193), `Microsoft-Windows-DXGI` Present events, and the `arbareto:history-painted` and `arbareto:changes-ready` marks. Confirm without change that the trace holds the mark and the present events of the WebView2 GPU process. Record the result in R6. Confirm that the trace and the injection records use the same counter time base. Before you use another preinstalled tool, check its presence without changes, and record its path and version in R6. Install no tool. A new install needs explicit maintainer approval, recorded in R6. Record each tool version and the display refresh rate. When the trace lacks the marks or the present events, leave the affected rows `unverified`. Also confirm the `arbareto:history-painted` mark with the `Arbareto.Light` level. Confirm the `toplevel` task events with the `Arbareto.Verbose` level (plan.md "Responsiveness metrics"). When no present tool meets a need, leave the affected SC-005, SC-006, or SC-015 rows `unverified`. Record the dedicated standard account that runs T166 to T169, T180, T181, and T245, and install the T154 package there. T242 records Δ of the capture source. When the T241 fallback already created `inject.ts` and its test, keep them and skip that step.
- [ ] T242 Create the test-only capture script `tests/windows/capture.ts` and its test `tests/windows/capture.test.ts` (plan.md "Capture source"). Write the test first; it must fail. The script calls D3D11 and DXGI through `bun:ffi` and needs no installation. It creates a D3D11 device on the adapter of the reference output and calls `IDXGIOutput1::DuplicateOutput`. It loops on `AcquireNextFrame` with a 100 ms time-out, within the run limit of its condition. `--limit <seconds>` sets that limit: 30 by default, 60 for C4 and C16, and 90 for C20 (plan.md "Run limit"). For each frame with a nonzero `LastPresentTime`, it copies the fixed region into a staging texture. It maps that texture, applies the mask, and hashes the bytes. It records `LastPresentTime`, `AccumulatedFrames`, and the hash, then releases the frame. It computes the settled frame, the end frame, and the run uncertainty u by the plan.md definitions. It saves the settled region as a lossless PNG through `node:zlib`. It marks each `capture-invalid` run with its cause and kind. It applies the plan.md "Invalid capture" limit of 3 added runs per block. A second repeatable failure with one cause ends the block. `--region <x,y,w,h>` sets the region, and C12 passes the list-pane rectangle of each application (plan.md "C12 region"). Record the Arbareto rectangle in research.md R6. It reads the refresh rate of the current output mode and prints Δ. `--calibrate` records the 5-second mask of one condition. `--launch <executable> [arguments]` records the QPC value just before it creates the process. The test builds a synthetic frame series in memory. It checks the settled frame, the end frame, u, the mask, and each invalid case. It also checks the added-run limit, the one-off and repeatable kinds, and the `unverified` result at the limit. A block that Arbareto runs end without a region change gives `missed`, and the same Git Extensions case gives `unverified`. An invalid warm-up run adds no run and counts toward both end rules. On the reference host, it also starts the installed app with `tests/windows/installed-app.ts`. It skips with a reason when the app is absent. It opens a fixture and sends Ctrl+1. Ctrl+1 leaves focus on the mode tab, so the T240 driver then moves focus to the message field. Under the T241 fallback, `inject.ts` sends Ctrl+1 and then F6 until the composer stop, as in T167. From the mode tabs, that takes four presses: Working changes, Staged, comparison, and composer. A notice band row adds one press (plan.md "Keyboard and focus", Pane navigation row). It injects one character with `inject.ts`. It asserts a region change whose `LastPresentTime` follows the injection value by at most 1 second. Under the fallback, the tester also confirms in the saved settled PNG that the character shows in the message field. Otherwise the live test records `unverified`. It asserts that no file under `src/` imports `capture.ts`. Record the output mode, Δ, and the adapter in research.md R6. `DuplicateOutput` can fail in a remote session or beside four duplicating processes. The live test then skips and names that capability (test harness rules). Record the HRESULT; the SC-015, SC-005, and SC-007 rows then stay `unverified`. Install no tool. Depends on T154, T165, and T240.
- [ ] T244 Create the test-only helper `tests/windows/external-change.ts` and its test `tests/windows/external-change.test.ts`. Write the test first; it must fail. `bun tests/windows/external-change.ts <file|index|ref> <fixture path>` records a QueryPerformanceCounter value just before it starts the change. It uses the QPC function that `inject.ts` exports; `inject.ts` calls `SendInput` only from its command-line entry, so the import injects nothing. It records a second value after the change completes. `--restore` returns the file, the index entry, or the ref to its state before the change. So each run starts from the same state. `--snapshot <fixture path>` records the start state of a mutation series beside the copy. The state holds the index file bytes, HEAD, every ref, and the working files that status lists. `--restore-state <fixture path>` writes that state back in place (plan.md "Restore start state"). It replaces the index file under an exclusively created `index.lock`. It sets HEAD and the refs with the matched Git executable. It writes file contents only and never recreates the top-level folder or the `.git` folder. A file change appends one line to a clean tracked text file of the fixture and closes the handle. An index change runs `git update-index --add` on that file with the matched Git executable. A ref change runs `git update-ref` on a test branch with the same executable. With `--wsl <distro>`, the helper makes the same change inside the distro through one `wsl.exe --exec` call. The start value then precedes that call. The helper prints both values and the kind. It refuses a path outside an `arbareto-fixtures-` root or a copy of the `arbareto-perf-fixtures` cache. The test runs each kind in an owned fixture. It asserts that the change happened and that the start value precedes the end value. It also takes a snapshot, stages a hunk, commits, and restores the state. Afterwards the index bytes, HEAD, refs, and listed working files equal the snapshot. The file IDs of the top-level folder and the `.git` folder stay unchanged. Depends on T165.

## Phase 10: Shell selection evidence and decision (Batch 6, cross-cutting)

**Purpose**: Decide from recorded evidence whether to retain Tauri. Scaffold no Electrobun, no second shell adapter, and no desktop abstraction layer.

- [ ] T172 When a cold-launch condition (C1, C13, or C17) fails in research.md R10, measure a minimal Tauri app. Use that condition's protocol and run count: ten cold runs for C1, and five for C13 or C17. Each run lasts from process creation after reboot to its first presented frame. Build it as T173 requires, outside the repository, and do not commit it. Record every run, the median, and that condition's target bound in research.md R10. When no cold-launch condition is fail, record that T172 does not apply.
- [ ] T173 Apply the plan.md shell-attribution rule to each `fail` row in research.md R10 that affects B-WINDOWS or B-PERFORMANCE. A failure is shell-attributable in three cases. A minimal Tauri app reproduces it. A trace leads into Tauri, wry, or WebView2 code. A documented shell capability is missing. Build each minimal reproduction in a disposable directory outside the repository, and do not commit it. Use the exact Tauri, wry, and tao versions from the committed `src-tauri/Cargo.lock`, and record them. Record the classification, the evidence path, and its digest. For an SC-015 cold-launch fail (C1, C13, or C17), use the T172 result, which applies only to those conditions. An SC-015 miss in any other condition is shell-attributable only through a trace or a documented missing capability.
- [ ] T174 For each shell-attributable failure, try the reasonable remedies that the constitution permits: a dependency version change, a configuration change, or a native workaround. Record each attempt, the dependency review for a version change, and the retest in research.md R10. Repeat the affected qualification checks after any dependency change.
- [ ] T175 Record the shell decision in research.md R1 as one line that starts with `Shell decision: `. State "retain Tauri" or the blocking requirement that the selected architecture cannot reasonably satisfy, and cite the R10 row IDs. State that no measurement compares Tauri with Electrobun, so no speed or superiority claim exists. Keep Electrobun source-only: add no adapter, no prototype, and no benchmark. Update the research.md R10 register with the evidence status of each group.

Request `https://api.github.com/repos/auric/arbareto/commits/<T154 build commit>` without a token. Record both results in research.md R7. B-RELEASE stays blocked until both succeed.

Pass condition: the first command prints exactly one line. That line states "retain Tauri" or a named blocking requirement, and it cites R10 row IDs. The second command prints nothing. Its word boundaries let the T175 phrase "superiority claim" pass. The third command prints one row whose current state says that public release is blocked. Control mutation: copy research.md to a scratch file outside the repository, and append the line `Arbareto is faster.` to the copy. The second command, run on the copy, must print that line. Delete the copy, and rerun the second command on research.md; it prints nothing. The task passes only after one independent review of batch 6 is recorded in research.md R10 "Batch reviews".

**Checkpoint**: Batch 6 is complete. The shell decision cites concrete results. Public release stays blocked.

### User story dependencies

- **US1**: Goes first. Its boot tasks provide the service session and Git selection (T061) that every other story needs.
- **US2**: Depends on US1 T061. Its snapshot, eligibility, and comparison tasks (T081, T084, T085, T105) block US3.
- **US3**: Depends on US2 T081, T084, T085, and T105.
- **US4**: Depends on US2 T080 and T081 and on the US3 exclusion, lock, and approval tasks (T106, T107, T108). It does not depend on hunk publication.
- **US5**: Its read parts depend on US2 T085 and T086. Its mutation parts depend on US3 T110 and US4 T113. Its UI parts depend on T128.
- **US6**: Its read parts depend on US2 T082, T083, T085, and T088. Its mutation parts depend on US3 T109, T110, and T200 and on US4 T112. Its UI parts depend on T128, T129, T132, and T137.
- **US7**: Depends on US2 T080 and T085, on US3 T106 and T108, and on US5 T113 and T114. Its WSL part depends on US6 T206, T211, and T212. Its UI parts depend on T117, T129, T133, and T138.

### Within each batch

- Tests come first and must fail.
- Contracts come before service code. Argument-array builders in `src/git-service/commands.ts` come before the modules that run Git.
- Service code comes before UI code. Mutation controls wait for T117.
- The batch verification task comes last. It passes only after one independent review of the batch is recorded in research.md R10 "Batch reviews". No automatic review loop runs.

## Implementation Strategy

### MVP first (batch 1: User Story 1 boot)

1. Complete Phase 1 and Phase 2.
2. Complete Phase 3.
3. **Stop and validate** with T065. The package installs, boots, admits prerequisites, and shows the environment.

### Incremental delivery

1. Read-only increment: Phase 4, then the US2 tasks of Phase 5 (T092, T093, T101 to T103, T105). Then the US1 and US2 read-view tasks of Phase 6. T105 also needs the contract module of T195 and T198. This gives inspection without mutation.
2. Mutation increment: the rest of Phase 5 through T117. Then the US3, US4, US5, and US7 tasks of Phase 6 through T149.
3. Qualification: Phases 7 to 9 through T171.
4. Decision: Phase 10 through T177.

WSL tasks (US6) ride along in batches 2 to 5. Their rows stay `unverified` until a WSL2 distro with Git 2.55.0 or later exists. That state blocks no Windows task.

Submodule tasks (US7) ride along in batches 3 to 5, after the mutation increment. T232 and T238 stay `unverified` under the same WSL condition.

The spec requires all seven stories for qualification. No partial increment counts as qualified. Public release stays blocked after T177.

---

## Notes

- `[P]` tasks touch different files and have no dependency on an incomplete task.
- `[Story]` labels map each task to a spec.md story for traceability.
- Verify that each test fails before its implementation task starts.
- The orchestrator session commits after each task or logical group. Never commit `src-tauri/binaries/*.exe`, fixture repositories, or performance fixtures.
- Stop at any checkpoint to validate the batch.
- Do not add Discard, Reset, force, repair, updater, Electrobun, a second shell adapter, a desktop abstraction, a custom task runner, or a planning tree.
